115 年 10 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
10 月出口 967.6 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 10.9%，較上
年同月增 58.9%(按新臺幣計算增 66.6%)；累 計 1 至 10 月出口 7,582.9
億美元，較上年同期增 47.7%(按新臺幣計算增 51.1%)。 
（二）進口 
10 月進口 688.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 8.3%，較上
年同月增 75.1%(按新臺幣計算增 83.5%)；累 計 1 至 10 月進口 5,697.6
億美元，較上年同期增 44.6%(按新臺幣計算增 47.6%)。 
（三）出入超 
10 月出超 278.8 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 63.3 億美元；
累計 1 至 10 月出超 1,885.4 億美元，較上年同期增 689.7 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續拓展，
加上適逢傳統外銷旺季，以及價格攀升效應，10 月出口較上年同月
增 358.7 億美元(+58.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 476.0 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增
200.7 億美元(+72.9%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增 151.6 億美元
(+67.5%)、電腦之零附件增 18.5 億美元(+1.0 倍)、儲存媒體增 17.3
億美元(+2.1 倍)、交換器及路由器增 11.7 億美元(+1.3 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 326.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 124.3
億美元(+61.4%)，其中積體電路增 120.5 億美元(+63.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.9 億美元(+38.5%)、電容器及電阻器增 1.2 億美元(+48.6%)。 
3. 基本金屬及其製品出口 28.8 億美元，較上年同月增 7.6 億美元
(+35.7%)，其中銅及其製品增 4.7 億美元(+1.2 倍)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.9 億美元(+16.4%)。 
4. 機械出口 28.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 7.5 億美元
(+35.5%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.9 億美元(+57.5%)、機器、
工廠或實驗室設備增 1.4 億美元(+70.0%)。 
5. 礦產品出口 15.5 億美元，較上年同月增 6.5 億美元(+71.6%)，其
中石油煉製品增 5.7 億美元(+72.2%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月出口較上年同期增 2,448.3 億美元(+47.7%)，以資通
與視聽產品增 1,423.3 億美元(+72.5%)、電子零組件增 850.5 億美元
(+47.5%)增加較多，礦產品、機械亦各增 41.9 億美元(+36.8%)、41.5
億美元(+19.8%)。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路、資通產品
及半導體設備進口持續強勁，加上國際原物料行情居高，10 月總進
口較上年同月增 295.5 億美元(+75.1%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口 458.3 億美元，較上年同月增 195.0 億美元(+74.0%)，
其中電子零組件增 136.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)、礦產品增 22.8 億美元
(+58.0%)、電腦之零附件增 16.2 億美元(+70.0%)、基本金屬增 6.2
億美元(+33.4%)、塑化原料增 5.0 億美元(+22.0%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 163.4 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通設備增 46.9 億美元(+2.0 倍)、半導體設備增 22.4 億美元
(+83.4%)、交通運輸設備增 7.6 億美元(+7.0 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 61.1 億美元，較上年同月增 18.8 億美元(+44.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.0 億美元(+5.3 倍)、食品增 0.5 億
美元(+5.1%)、小客車增 0.4 億美元(+10.2%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月對美國、歐洲、日本出口創新高 
1. 對美國出口 329.3 億美元，較上年同月增 118.1 億美元(+55.9%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 98.3 億美元(+56.6%)、電子零組件增 13.5
億美元(+1.7 倍)、機械增 2.4 億美元(+41.6%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 204.4 億美元，較上年同月增 61.4 億美元
(+42.9%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+48.0%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.6 億美元(+36.5%)、基本金屬及其製品增 3.2 億美元
(+95.2%)。 
3. 對東協出口 184.6 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+83.8%)，
其中電子零組件增 38.6 億美元(+75.5%)、資通與視聽產品增 36.5
億美元(+1.4 倍)、礦產品增 5.0 億美元(+2.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 59.5 億美元，較上年同月增 18.7 億美元(+46.0%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 10.7 億美元(+47.9%)、電子零組件增 3.3 億
美元(+81.3%)、機械增 1.2 億美元(+50.8%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.7 億美元，較上年同月增 12.2 億美元(+49.6%)，其
中電子零組件增 7.5 億美元(+63.2%)、資通與視聽產品增 3.2 億美
元(+1.3 倍)、化學品增 0.5 億美元(+47.0%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月對美國出口 2,407.1 億美元，較上年同期增 891.1 億美
元(+58.8%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,805.3 億美元，增 416.6 億美
元(+30.0%)，對東協出口 1,524.1 億美元，增 547.6 億美元(+56.1%)，
對歐洲出口 481.3 億美元，增 157.1 億美元(+48.5%)，對日本出口 314.4
億美元，增 70.5 億美元(+28.9%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月自東協進口 134.1 億美元，較上年同月增 82.3 億美元(+1.6 倍)，
自南韓進口 130.0 億 美 元，增 73.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)，自中國大陸與香
港進口 128.3 億美元，增 51.9 億美元(+67.9%)，自日本進口 62.0 億
美元，增 18.8 億美元(+43.4%)，自美國進口 53.6 億美元，增 14.7 億
美元(+37.9%)，自歐洲進口 48.2 億美元，增 9.5 億美元(+24.6%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較，自東協增 85.6%、
自南韓增 72.7%、自中國大陸與香港增 45.3%、自日本增 27.4%、自
美國增 23.0%、自歐洲增 12.1%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-9 月主要國家/地區出口均呈上升，其中南韓(1-10 月)、香港、
新加坡受 AI 相關需求帶動科技供應鏈及區域貿易活動熱絡，累計出
口分別年增 60.6%、52.3%、41.3%；中國大陸、美國各年增 21.3%
及 14.3%，德國(1-8 月)、日本各增 8.8%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步成長，累計 1-9 月以香港進口年增 51.8%較
高，新加坡、中國大陸、南 韓 (1-10 月)分別年增 39.0%、27.5%及 21.4%，
德國(1-8 月)、日本、美國各增 8.4%、8.2%、6.7%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）隨全球人工智慧(AI)基礎建設投資強勁成長，帶動相關供應鏈需求擴
增，加以外銷產品價格攀升，10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，首度突破
900 億美元，年增 58.9%，連續 36 個月正成長；進口 688.9 億美元，
同為歷年單月最高，年增 75.1%，主要受惠於 AI 產業之國際分工與
出口引申需求增加，以及國際原物料行情高漲；出超 278.8 億美元亦
創新高。累計 1 至 10 月出口年增 47.7%、進口增 44.6%。
（二）10 月出口主要貨品中，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件出口值雙創單月
新高，各年增 72.9%及 61.4%，主因 AI、高效能運算及雲端服務等應
用商機熱絡；其餘貨類出口平均增 25.7%，其中礦產品因油價支撐增
71.6%，機械、電機產品受惠於半導體業者擴充產能及 AI 需求外溢，
增幅皆達 3 成，基本金屬及其製品亦增 35.7%。累 計 1 至 10 月資通與
視聽產品、電子零組件合占總出口比重約 8 成，年增 60.6%。

（三）10 月對主要市場出口年增率均逾 4 成，以對東協增 83.8%較突出，
對美國、日本、歐洲出口規模皆為單月新高，各增 55.9%、49.6%及
46.0%，對中國大陸與香港增 42.9%。累 計 1 至 10 月對五大市場出口
增幅介於 28%~59%，其中對美國出口年增 58.8%、對東協增 56.1%，
占總出口比重各為 31.7%及 20.1%，分別寫下近 36 年同期及歷年同
期新高紀錄。
（四）展望未來，全球經濟仍面臨地緣政治衝突、美國貿易政策走向等不
確定因素，惟隨人工智慧應用逐漸擴散至推論、代理式 AI，且國際
雲端服務業者持續布建 AI 基礎設施，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供
應鏈需求延續，加以科技新品相繼推 出、年底消費旺季來臨之挹注，
我國第 4 季出口可望維持強勁成長態勢。

### Page 1

115 年 9 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
9 月出口 872.2 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.9%，較上年
同月增 60.9%(按新臺幣計算增 68.2%)；第 3 季出口 2,449.2 億美元，
較上年同季增 44.6%；累計 1 至 9 月 6,615.5 億美元，較上年同期增
46.2%(按新臺幣計算增 49.1%)。 
（二）進口 
9 月進口 635.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.8%，較上
年同月增 51.7%(按新臺幣計算增 58.6%)；第 3 季進口 1,818.0 億美
元，較上年同季增 44.3%；累計 1 至 9 月 5,010.0 億美元，較上年同
期增 41.3%(按新臺幣計算增 43.8%)。 
（三）出入超 
9 月出超 236.3 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 113.4 億美元；
累計 1 至 9 月出超 1,605.5 億美元，較上年同期增 625.4 億美元。

### Page 2

2 
二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於創新科技應用商機持續擴展，推升相關供應鏈出貨動能，加上
消費性電子新品備貨需求與產品價格上漲效應之挹注，9 月出口較
上年同月增 330.0 億美元(+60.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 391.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增
198.6 億美元(+1.0 倍)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增 157.4 億美元(+1.1
倍)、儲存媒體增 17.6 億美元(+2.2 倍)、交換器及路由器增 10.5
億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 311.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增 97.1
億美元(+45.3%)，其中積體電路增 93.9 億美元(+46.1%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+33.7%)、電容器及電阻器增 0.8 億美元(+32.0%)。 
3. 礦產品出口 22.6 億美元，較上年同月增 10.5 億美元(+86.4%)，其
中石油煉製品增 10.1 億美元(+99.0%)。 
4. 機械出口 27.4 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增 6.4 億美元
(+30.7%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.7 億美元(+61.0%)、未列名
具特殊功能機械增 0.6 億美元(+73.6%)。 
5. 基本金屬及其製品出口 26.9 億美元，較上年同月增 5.0 億美元
(+22.9%)，其中銅及其製品增 2.5 億美元(+65.1%)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.4 億美元(+11.7%)。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈全球分工與出口引申需求擴增，電子零組件、資通產品
及半導體設備進口維持強勁，加上國際農工原料行情處於高檔，9
月進口較上年同月增 216.7 億美元(+51.7%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口 440.5 億美元，較上年同月增 163.9 億美元(+59.3%)，
其中電子零組件增 112.6 億美元(+82.5%)、電腦之零附件增 19.4
億美元(+1.5 倍)、礦產品增 17.1 億美元(+38.9%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 128.0 億美元，較上年同月增 36.0 億美元(+39.1%)，
其中資通設備增 18.4 億美元(+75.0%)、半導體設備增 12.4 億美元
(+34.9%)，交通運輸設備減 1.4 億美元(-46.4%)。 
3. 消費品進口 62.1 億美元，較上年同月增 16.5 億美元(+36.0%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.4 億美元(+5.3 倍)、筆電增 0.5 億
美元(+43.8%)，藥粧用品減 1.2 億美元(-16.6%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月對東協出口創新高，對美國、陸港為單月次高 
1. 對美國出口 274.6 億美元，較上年同月增 141.5 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 124.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)、電子零組件增 10.1
億美元(+1.3 倍)、機械增 2.2 億美元(+42.3%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 206.2 億美元，較上年同月增 47.8 億美元
(+30.2%)，其中電子零組件增 34.9 億美元(+30.5%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.7 億美元(+39.0%)、機械增 1.9 億美元(+43.7%)。 
3. 對東協出口 188.8 億美元，較上年同月增 91.2 億美元(+93.4%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 49.7 億美元(+2.2 倍)、電子零組件增 32.0
億美元(+63.3%)、礦產品增 4.8 億美元(+1.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 45.3 億美元，較上年同月增 7.7 億美元(+20.6%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 5.7 億美元(+34.1%)、電子零組件增 2.2 億美
元(+56.6%)，基本金屬及其製品減 0.8 億美元(-20.5%)。 
5. 對日本出口 30.7 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+13.4%)，其
中電子零組件增 1.5 億美元(+11.0%)、基本金屬及其製品增 0.6 億
美元(+32.5%)、機械增 0.5 億美元(+29.1%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月對美國出口 2,077.8 億美元，較上年同期增 773.1 億美
元(+59.3%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,600.9 億美元，增 355.3 億美
元(+28.5%)，對東協出口 1,339.6 億美元，增 463.6 億美元(+52.9%)，
對歐洲出口 421.8 億美元，增 138.4 億美元(+48.8%)，對日本出口 277.7
億美元，增 58.3 億美元(+26.6%)。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）主要國家/地區今年 1-8 月外貿景氣普遍走升，以南韓(1-9 月)出口年
增 56.8%最為強勁，香港、新加坡亦分別年增 49.0%、39.8%；中國
大陸、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-7 月)、日本各增
9.7%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-8 月以香港進口年增 50.5%較
突出，新加坡、中國大陸分別年增 37.3%、27.0%，南韓(1-9 月)、德
國(1-7 月)分別增 19.7%、10.0%，日本、美國各增 7.8%、4.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續擴展，
加以步入傳統外銷旺季、產品價格上漲效應，9 月出口 872.2 億美元，
年增 60.9%，連續 35 個月正成長；進口 635.9 億美元，年增 51.7%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口引申需求擴張，及國際原物料行情居
高；出、進口值及出超 236.3 億美元齊創單月新高。累計 1 至 9 月出
口年增 46.2%、進口增 41.3%。
（二）9 月主要貨品出口普遍較上年同月成長，以資通與視聽產品增 1.0 倍
最佳，包括顯示卡、伺服器、儲存媒體、交換器及路由器等出口均
呈倍數增加；其次為礦產品增 86.4%，主因全球油品供應偏緊支撐價
格維持高檔；電子零組件隨 AI 應用及消費性電子新品備貨需求升溫，
增 45.3%，機械、電機產品亦各增 3 成上下。累計 1 至 9 月資通與視
聽產品、電子零組件出口併計增 59.5%，占總出口比重 79.0%。

（三）主要市場方面，9 月對美國、東協出口值分創歷年單月次高、新高，
年增率各為 1.1 倍及 93.4%，合計對總出口增加貢獻達 7 成；對中國
大陸與香港、歐洲、日本出口各年增 30.2%、20.6%、13.4%。累計 1
至 9 月對五大市場出口值皆為歷年同期最高，其中對美國、東協均
提前刷新全年紀錄，分別年增 59.3%、52.9%，對歐洲出口年增 48.8%，
對陸港、日本各增 28.5%、26.6%。
（四）展望未來，全球貿易政策及地緣政治情勢變化等，仍增添總體經濟
不確定性，惟隨人工智慧應用持續深化，國際雲端服務業者及各國
加速 AI 基礎建設投資，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供應鏈需求擴增，
加以科技新品備貨、中國大陸及歐美年底採購旺季將屆，均有利挹
注我國外銷動能，第 4 季出口可望延續成長態勢。

MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」 直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market）」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於 2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至「邊境市場（Frontier Market
）」，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但 MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但 MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低

觸及 4,907.37 點。雖然 20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度下跌 1.22%
，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」，原則上將不再納入 MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外。相關改革有助改

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續，以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證，我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至 2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異，區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響，以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)
3Q26 Beat on Margins Reinforces AI Thermal Ramp; TP Maintained at 
NT$4,350
CITI'S TAKE 
AVC reported net profit of NT$11.6bn, topping Coto/cons estimates by 
2%/2% respectively, driven by margins beat partly offset by a non-ops FX 
loss. GPM improvement was above expectations on a richer liquid cooling 
mix, yield gains and disciplined raw material pass-through. Mgmt. guided 
positively for 4Q26 and 2027, led by AI ASIC ramp-up and Vera Rubin ramp 
up. We model 16% QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26E. GPM guidance also 
points to further sequential improvement on a higher liquid cooling mix and 
scale effects. Mgmt. reiterated 2026E/27E capex of NT$15bn/15-20bn, 
alongside 50%+ cold plate capacity expansion, underscoring capacity 
conviction. We fine-tune our earnings forecasts to reflect updated guidance 
and maintain TP at NT$4,350 (25x 2027E EPS). Reiterate Buy.
3Q26 results beat on margins — AVC’s reported 3Q26 net profit of NT$11.6bn 
(+20% QoQ/+52% YoY) beat Citi/cons forecast by 2%/2%, driven by better-than-
expected margins, partly offset by a non-ops loss. 3Q26 GPM came in at 33.9% 
(+1.3ppts QoQ/+7.7 ppts YoY), above both Citi/cons estimates, mainly due to 
continued mix improvement, a higher liquid cooling contribution, yield gains, and 
disciplined pass-through of raw material price fluctuations to customers. Non-ops 
included a NT$304mn FX loss, which partially offset the strong core business.
ASIC ramp and capacity guidance underpin long-term outlook — Mgmt. guided 
positively for 4Q26 and 2027, led by AI ASIC project ramp-up and early Vera Rubin 
generation contribution. We now model 16% QoQ revenue growth for 4Q26E. GPM 
guidance also points to further sequential improvement vs. 3Q26, driven by a higher 
liquid cooling mix and scale effects. Mgmt. expects server compute tray liquid 
cooling penetration to reach 100% by end-2027. Mgmt. reiterated 2026E/26E capex 
guidance of NT$15bn/15-20bn, alongside 50%+ cold plate capacity expansion, 
reinforcing confidence in capacity build-out.
Implications — We fine-tune our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts to reflect updated 
guidance and outlook. We maintain our TP at NT$4,350, still based on 25x 2027E EPS. 
We believe AVC’s solid supply position across key customers’ AI GPU and ASIC projects, 
combined with a sustained growth trajectory, continues to support its valuation. With 
visibility extending into 2027, we see limited near-term read-through risk from the 
ongoing platform transition. We reiterate our Buy rating on AVC.

### Page 6

Asia Vital Components (3017.TW)
10 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Asia Vital Components
Company description
Asia Vital Components (AVC), founded in 1991, is a global leader in thermal
solutions, originally focusing on PCs but now specializing in servers and
networking. With Furukawa Electric (Japan) as its largest shareholder, AVC
provides a comprehensive range of cooling components, including vapor
chambers (VCs), fans, cold plates, CDUs, and manifolds, as well as chassis

and assembly services for PCs and servers, enabling holistic thermal solution
designs and one-stop shop services. AVC's diverse product portfolio supports
various sectors, including consumer IT, cloud infrastructure, transportation,
energy and networking.
Investment strategy
We rate AVC a Buy. AVC is well-positioned for the liquid-cooling transition,
with leadership in cold plates and broad participation across Nvidia and CSP
projects. Its high-volume machining capability, manufacturing uniformity,

and Vietnam-based capacity provide it with a defensible position as
Blackwell and future deployments expand. The company captures a wider
share of the thermal BOM, across coldplates, manifolds, QDs, and CDU
opportunities, providing a multi-layered earnings engine.
Valuation
We value AVC using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum
and the expansion of its addressable market. Our TP of NT$4,350 is derived
from applying a 25x PE multiple on 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified

given the company’s entrenched positioned in the server thermal segment,
rising contribution from liquid cooling, and the increasing value of thermal
content per server as power density continues to climb. With these structural
drivers still in place, we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its
historical averages.
Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model but assigning a
High Risk rating is not supported by other qualitative factors such as cold-

plate leadership and deep CSP penetration, and so a High Risk rating has not
been applied. Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target price
include: 1) slower adoption pace for liquid cooling adoption; 2) heightened
price competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling
components; and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: AVC announced its 3Q26 results and hosted an investor 
conference on 10 November 2026 after market hours. We believe AVC will 
be one of the key beneficiaries from the rising penetration rate of liquid 
cooling in both ASIC and GPU projects, with an aggressive capacity 
expansion plan over 2026-28. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call (see our latest 
Flash, 3Q26 results preview: likely to beat expectations, on 2 November).

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results beat the Bloomberg consensus. 
AVC’s 3Q26 revenue of TWD59,089m (+20.3% QoQ; +51.8% YoY) was 
roughly in line with our and the Bloomberg consensus estimates. Its 3Q26 
gross margin came in at 33.9% (vs. 32.6% in 2Q26 and 26.1% in 3Q25), a 
record-high level, higher than our estimate (33.5%) and the consensus 
(33.3%) on better product mix and high server revenue contribution of 66% 
in 3Q26 vs. 66% in 2Q26 and 54% in 3Q25. Its operating profit of 
TWD16,856m (+25.1% QoQ; +108.5% YoY) was in line with our estimate 
and beat the consensus by 4.2%. Thus, its operating margin of 28.5% (vs. 
27.4% in 2Q26 and 20.8% in 3Q25), a record-high level, was also in line 
with our estimate and beat the consensus (27.9%). With an FX loss of 
TWD304m, its net profit of TWD11,564m (+20.9% QoQ; +116.6% YoY) was 
in line with our estimate and beat the market consensus estimate by 3%. Its 
3Q26 EPS finished at TWD29.32.

Key highlights from the investor conference – liquid cooling revenue 
from AI servers continue to rise. AVC is confident it can maintain its 
leading position as a thermal and mechanical solution integrator, being a 
primary design partner for ASIC and GPU projects. In 2027, AVC expects 
its revenue from ASICs projects to be higher than GPU ones. From 3Q26, 
AVC has started shipping the double-sided cold plates, which it expects to 
be adopted by all ASIC clients and some GPU clients. With years of 
experience in providing CDU parts, AVC has also started shipping complete 
CDU solutions from 3Q26. AVC aims for TWD15-20bn capex in 2027 
(mainly in Vietnam, for liquid cooling capacity expansion), up from 
TWD15bn in 2026E. We keep our 2026-28E EPS broadly unchanged.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call and 12M TP of 
TWD4,815, still based on a target PER of 30x vs. its past-3-year PER 
range of 5-25x [average PER of 15x]) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS. 
Downside risks: slower-than-expected progress in high-gross-margin 
orders, slow AI server adoption, and strong competition.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 8% above consensus, likely on our 
more aggressive gross- and operating margins.

10 November 2026

3Q26 review: liquid cooling penetration rate rises

➢ 3Q26 EPS beat the Bloomberg consensus by 3% 
➢ Started shipments of double-sided cold plates and CDUs from 3Q26 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call with an unchanged 12M TP of TWD4,815

Company profile 
AVC is a global leading comprehensive thermal solutions provider in segments related to desktops, 
laptops, communication, servers, energy and autos. Its main product line includes thermal products 
(thermal modules and fans), chassis, system assembly and peripheral products and hinges. The 
company has been listed on Taiwan’s main board since September 2002.

Asia Vital Components (3017 TT): 10 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 6 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson of AVC are the same individual, but we see no issue with its board
quality. Besides, we see the Chairperson has 24 years of experience in this industry, which is a
positive sign from an executive perspective.
Capital management 2 AVC's dividend payout ratio was 40-52% over 2020-25, likely due to its capacity expansion plans.
Related party &
transaction 2 Revenue generated from related-party transactions was 1-3% of total revenue during 2021-25, and we found no suspicious activity.
E Supply chain management 1
AVC has introduced Hazardous Substance Process Management (HSPM) and received
QC080000 certification. In addition, AVC set up a Green Product team to promote HSPM and
formulate environmental regulation to make sure its material sourcing and efficiency are well-
performed.
E Data security 1
To prevent the interruption of information systems and leakage of sensitive information causing
losses to the company and customers, AVC has introduced an information security management
system and passed the ISO 27001 information security management system certification in
2019.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
3Q26 Beat on Margins from ASP Pass-Through and Richer Mix
CITI'S TAKE 
MSI reported 3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), 
beating Citi/cons by 27%/45% on stronger-than-expected margins and 
non-op gains. GPM of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8 ppts YoY) topped Citi/cons 
at 15.8%/14.5%, as ASP hikes passed through rising component costs while 
moving-avg inventory costs lagged, alongside a richer high-end product 
mix. For 4Q26E, we expect further ASP increases and a rising server 
contribution to largely offset seasonal shipment declines. Into 2027, we 
expect PC demand to stay weak and pass-through room to narrow as 
memory price increases moderately. We raise 2026E-28E earnings and our 
TP to NT$145 (10x 2027E EPS). We stay Neutral as the share price already 
reflects the pass-through upside.
3Q26: Margin-led beat on cost pass-through and better mix — MSI reported 
3Q26 net profit of NT$4.0bn (+34% QoQ/+138% YoY), beating Citi/cons forecasts 
by 27%/45%, mainly on stronger-than-expected margins and non-op gains. GPM 
of 17.0% (+1.1ppts QoQ/+5.8ppts YoY) came in above Citi/cons at 15.8%/14.5%. 
Mgmt. attributed the upside to 1) ASP increase, as MSI raised prices in line with 
suppliers’ cost increases while its moving-average inventory costs rose less; and 2) 
a higher mix of high-end products. Non-op gains, including FX gains of over 
NT$100mn, further boosted the bottom line.
Price hikes to continue; 2027 PC market to keep shrinking — We reiterate our view 
that 4Q26 shipments will decline seasonally from the 3Q26 peak. However, mgmt. 
expects rising raw material costs and continued pass-through to keep lifting ASPs, 
offsetting lower volumes and making the seasonal decline milder than in prior years. 
The server business should also contribute more, potentially reaching a high-single-
digit revenue share. Into 2027, we expect weak PC demand amid elevated costs, 
with MSI’s MB and PC shipments declining by over 10%. Pass-through room should 
narrow as memory price increases moderately, though margins should stay elevated 
for a while, as mgmt. expects memory supply to stay tight in the coming quarters.
Implications — We raise our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts by 6-27%, reflecting 
sustained strong component cost pass-through and higher margin assumptions. 
We adjust TP to NT$145 (from NT$140), implying 10x 2027E EPS (from 12x). We 
believe the share price already reflects MSI’s opportunity to pass component costs 
through to end products, and we expect limited upside going forward, given that the 
PC demand recovery remains highly uncertain. Maintain Neutral.

### Page 6

Micro-Star (2377.TW)

Micro-Star
Company description
Established in 1986 and listed in 1998, Taiwan-based MSI is a leading
OBM/ODM for gaming PCs, motherboards, graphic cards, industrial PCs, and
servers. MSI started business as a motherboard manufacturer but
transformed itself to become a leading gaming PC play in 2012 with a
comprehensive product portfolio across the gaming system and components.
MSI enjoys about 16% global share in the gaming NB industry, and is ranked

No.3 in graphic card shipments and No.3 in motherboard shipments globally.
Investment strategy
We rate MSI shares at Neutral as we anticipate earnings upward pressure
from the delince in PC and MB/VGA shipments in the near-term. While the
soaring component costs could be partially offset by price hike, we expect
muted demand and AI resource crowding to wiegh on margins upside. Further
upside maintains limited, though, until we see a meaningful recovery in core

gaming demand or a more substantial contribution from the server business.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$145 is based on 10x 2027E EPS, below the stock's five-
year average forward 12-month PE. This valuation reflects the earnings
downside risks from muted PC demand and margin pressure from soaring
component costs, while accounting for the time required for the server
business to achieve meaningful scale.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause MSI's shares to trade above our target price

include: 1) a sharper-than-expected recovery in high-end gaming demand;
and 2) faster-than-anticipated revenue contribution from the AI server
segment. Key downside risks include: 1) a deeper-than-expected contraction
in global PC shipments; 2) prolonged supply shortages for gaming GPUs due
to Ai resource crowding; and 3) inability to pass through surging memory and
CPU costs to price-sensitive consumers.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Document 194: 4连涨！短期美债收益率创2022年以来最快涨幅，市场押注美联储加息

Source PDF: all report pdf/4连涨！短期美债收益率创2022年以来最快涨幅，市场押注美联储加息.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

美国短期国债收益率连续四个交易⽇⼤幅攀升， 2 年期收益率升⾄ 4.862% ，累计涨幅 38.8
个基点，创 2022 年 6 ⽉以来最⼤四⽇涨幅。 10 年期收益率同步突破 5.10% 关键阈值。实际
利率同步攀升，市场已将终端利率预期⼤幅上调⾄ 4.82% ， 12 ⽉加息基本已成定局， 1 ⽉再
度加息的概率则⾼达 75% 。
摘要：
美国短期国债收益率在四个交易⽇内完成了⾃ 2022 年加息周期以来最⼤幅度的跳升，市场正在
重新定价美联储政策预期 —— 押注利率将更⾼、更持久。
周四，对美联储政策最为敏感的 2 年期美债收益率升⾄ 4.862% ，过去四个交易⽇（不计周三退
伍军⼈节休市）累计上⾏ 38.8 个基点，创下⾃ 2022 年 6 ⽉ 15 ⽇以来最⼤四交易⽇涨幅。
与此同时， 10 年期美债收益率也突破 5.10% ，刷新⼼理关键阈值，对美股构成压⼒。
此轮抛售的导⽕索接连引爆： 10 ⽉美国 CPI 数据⾼于预期、劳动⼒市场依然⽕热，美联储理事沃
勒周⼆暗⽰ 12 ⽉或需加息 50 个基点，克利夫兰联储主席 Hammack 重申政策须转向限制性⽴场，

加之地缘政治升温推⾼油价，三重压⼒叠加，加速了国债市场的抛售。
TD Securities 美国利率策略主管 Gennadiy Goldberg 表⽰：
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

通胀黏性与鹰派官员合⼒施压
周四引发市场震荡的，是多重因素的密集共振。
其⼀， 10 ⽉ CPI 数据的强劲令市场担忧通胀依然⼴泛且具黏性，进⽽质疑美联储当前渐进式加息
节奏是否⾜以应对通胀压⼒。
其⼆，多位美联储官员持续释放鹰派信号，克利夫兰联储主席 Hammack 上⽉曾投票⽀持加息，
当天重申货币政策必须转⼊限制性区间，⽅能将通胀压回 2% ⽬标。
其三，地缘政治局势的新进展推升油价，进⼀步强化了通胀⻛险叙事，加速了国债抛售。
三重因素叠加，当⽇美债收益率单⽇上⾏ 12 ⾄ 13 个基点，其中⻓端表现相对抗跌，但 10 年期收
益率仍上⾏约 8 个基点；⼀场疲弱的 30 年期国债标售结果更是雪上加霜，令⻓端收益率进⼀步承
压， 10 年期收益率最终升破 5.10% 。
实际利率同步攀升，加息预期⼤幅上移
此轮债市重定价幅度显著。
TD Securities 美国利率策略主管 Gennadiy Goldberg 认为，交易员周四已将 " 终端利率 " 预期从数
⽇前的 4.44% ⼤幅上调⾄ 4.82% ，并开始押注美联储在明年 1 ⽉前将累计再加息两次 —— 其中 12

⽉加息基本已成定局， 1 ⽉再度加息的概率则⾼达 75% 。
此次收益率急升本质上是市场对美联储预期重新定价的结果，反映出未来数⽉更为鹰派的政策⾛
向。

### Page 3

美联储今年 9 ⽉时隔三年⾸度重启加息，将联邦基⾦利率⽬标区间上调⾄ 3.75% ⾄ 4% 。
此前⼀轮⾃ 2022 年初启动、从接近零利率出发的激进加息周期，曾将利率上限推⾄ 2023 ⾄ 2024
年间的 5.5% 峰值，彼时的加息节奏为 1987 年以来最快。
值得注意的是，此轮收益率上⾏并⾮单纯由通胀预期驱动。10 年期通胀保值债券（ TIPS ）实际
收益率周四同样⼤幅跳升，连续第五个交易⽇上⾏，年初⾄今累计涨幅达 90 个基点。
分析认为，实际收益率的同步攀升意味着，市场的抛售逻辑不仅仅是对通胀的担忧，更折射出
投资者对美联储将实施真实货币紧缩的坚定预期。这对⻛险资产的压⼒，或将较单纯的通胀预
期升温更为持久。
沃什⻛格下的⾼波动时代
此轮债市动荡，也是美联储主席沃什上任以来市场⽣态转变的缩影。
沃什于今年 5 ⽉接任美联储主席⼀职，主张联储减少前瞻性沟通、更多地跟随市场信号⾏事，其
⻛格与前任形成鲜明对⽐。他还计划明年减少⼀次联邦公开市场委员会议息会议。
这种更为低调、被动的沟通⽅式，已被市场解读为政策不确定性上升的信号，并在⼀定程度上

放⼤了债市对于数据和官员⾔论的价格敏感性。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

⾏倒闭，并引发了 2023 年那场短暂⽽剧烈的区域性银⾏业危机。
如今，随着收益率再度逼近历史⾼位，市场对利率冲击的潜在连锁效应，正重新提⾼警觉。

레인 대응 전략 유지, 내부 원가 및 체질 개선으로 외부 비용 부담 상쇄를

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh

Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu 
Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích 
FRT 148.70 1.2% 143.40 165.00 15.1%

Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm 
- Dư địa lấy thêm thị phần từ các cửa hàng nhỏ lẻ vẫn còn tương đối rộng, FRT đặt mục tiêu 
chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại

Document 301: Cerebras大涨！Q3营收超预期，云业务激增272%，上调全年指引｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cerebras大涨！Q3营收超预期，云业务激增272%，上调全年指引｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Cerebras 三季度核⼼营收 2.3 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 70% ，⾼于市场预期的 2.15 亿美元；其
中，云服务收⼊同⽐增⻓ 272% ⾄ 1.45 亿美元。公司上调 2026 年全年核⼼营收指引，从此
前的 8.8 亿⾄ 8.9 亿美元上调⾄ 9.4 亿⾄ 9.6 亿美元，预计第四季度核⼼营收将达到 3.1 亿⾄
3.3 亿美元。 Cerebras 股价盘后上涨 16% 。
摘要：
AI 推理需求持续升温， AI 芯⽚公司 Cerebras 第三季度业绩超出市场预期，云业务收⼊成为增⻓
的核⼼引擎。公司不仅上调了 2026 年全年营收指引，还预计 2027 年营收将达到今年的三倍，显
⽰其对 AI 推理算⼒需求的增⻓前景持乐观态度。
当地时间 11 ⽉ 12 ⽇，Cerebras 公布 2026 年第三季度财报。公司当季核⼼营收达到 2.309 亿美
元，同⽐增⻓ 70% ，⾼于分析师平均预期的 2.147 亿美元；其中，云及其他服务收⼊同⽐增⻓
272% ⾄ 1.449 亿美元。

受云业务增⻓和客⼾需求推动，Cerebras 将 2026 年全年核⼼营收指引从此前的 8.8 亿⾄ 8.9 亿美
元上调⾄ 9.4 亿⾄ 9.6 亿美元。公司还预计，第四季度核⼼营收将达到 3.1 亿⾄ 3.3 亿美元，核⼼⽑
利率升⾄ 47% ⾄ 49% 。
订单储备⽅⾯，截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇， Cerebras 剩余履约义务（ RPO ）达到 261 亿美元。公司同时披
露，已经签约 700 兆⽡的数据中⼼容量，计划在 2027 年底前交付。
不过，强劲的营收增⻓尚未转化为 GAAP ⼝径下的盈利改善。第三季度公司净亏损扩⼤⾄ 1.612
亿美元，上年同期为 2220 万美元。随着公司加快芯⽚制造和数据中⼼扩张，股权激励、客⼾认
股权证摊销等项⽬也使 GAAP 与核⼼⼝径的经营表现出现明显差异。
财报公布后， Cerebras 股价在盘后交易中上涨 16% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

云业务收⼊激增，硬件销售并⾮唯⼀增⻓引擎
从收⼊结构来看， Cerebras 正在扩⼤云端算⼒服务的业务规模。第三季度，公司 GAAP ⼝径云及
其他服务收⼊达到 1.419 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 265% ；核⼼⼝径下，该项收⼊为 1.449 亿美元，同⽐
增⻓ 272% ，约占核⼼总营收的 63% 。
相⽐之下，硬件业务收⼊表现相对疲弱。第三季度 GAAP 硬件收⼊为 6888 万美元，低于去年同
期的 9679 万美元。按照核⼼⼝径计算，硬件收⼊为 8596 万美元，也低于去年同期的 9679 万美
元。
这⼀变化反映出 Cerebras 的收⼊结构正在向云服务倾斜。公司不再仅仅依赖出售 AI 芯⽚和计算
系统，还通过⾃营云平台及第三⽅云服务商提供推理算⼒，以扩⼤客⼾获取算⼒的渠道。
不过，硬件收⼊下降并不意味着公司正在主动放弃硬件销售。CEO Andrew Feldman 的表态
称，硬件销售受到交付时间不均衡的影响，⽽⾮公司战略转向云业务。
OpenAI 、微软和 Oracle 扩⼤合作，算⼒订单储备达 261 亿美元

⼤型客⼾合作正在成为 Cerebras 扩张的重要⽀撑。
Cerebras 与 OpenAI 达成了⼀项多年期、价值约 200 亿美元的算⼒供应协议。公司正在扩⼤芯⽚
供应规模，以满⾜相关协议及其他客⼾的算⼒需求。
微软已成为 Cerebras 的新客⼾，现有合作伙伴还包括 Oracle 和 OpenAI 。 Oracle 此前宣布，将
Cerebras 的⾼速 AI 推理能⼒整合进 Oracle Cloud Infrastructure ，相关服务预计于 2027 年上线。
订单储备⽅⾯，截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇， Cerebras 剩余履约义务（ RPO ）达到 261 亿美元。公司同时披
露，已经签约 700 兆⽡的数据中⼼容量，计划在 2027 年底前交付。
此外， Cerebras 还宣布与⼀家全球前三⼤超⼤规模云服务商达成新的云业务合作，但尚未披露
客⼾名称。公司表⽰，相关信息将在明年进⼀步公布。
这些合作意味着， Cerebras 正在通过直接销售、云平台和第三⽅云服务商等多种渠道扩⼤商业

覆盖范围。对于公司⽽⾔，能否按计划交付数据中⼼容量并将订单储备转化为实际收⼊，将成
为未来增⻓的重要观察指标。
上调全年指引，预计 2027 年营收增⻓⾄今年三倍
在第三季度业绩超预期后， Cerebras 进⼀步上调了全年业绩展望。
公司⽬前预计，2026 年核⼼营收将达到 9.4 亿⾄ 9.6 亿美元，⾼于此前预计的 8.8 亿⾄ 8.9 亿美元。
第四季度核⼼营收预计为 3.1 亿⾄ 3.3 亿美元，核⼼⽑利率预计达到 47% ⾄ 49% ，核⼼营业利润率
预计在 -1% ⾄ 1% 之间。
对于 2027 年， CEO Andrew Feldman 给出了更为积极的增⻓预期，预计公司营收将达到 2026
年的三倍。
为⽀撑这⼀⽬标， Cerebras 正在加快制造和基础设施扩张。公司预计，到 2026 年底，制造产能
将超过年初的 10 倍，并计划在 2027 年进⼀步增加⼯⼚和⽣产能⼒。

在资⾦⽅⾯，公司披露，截⾄第三季度末，现⾦、现⾦等价物、受限现⾦及短期投资合计达到
78 亿美元，另有 8.5 亿美元债务融资能⼒。
GAAP 亏损扩⼤，增⻓质量仍待检验
尽管营收和订单储备表现强劲， Cerebras 的盈利状况仍值得关注。
第三季度，公司 GAAP 净亏损达到 1.612 亿美元，较去年同期的 2220 万美元明显扩⼤； GAAP 营
业亏损达到 2.241 亿美元。相⽐之下，公司核⼼营业利润率为 -14.3% ，仍未实现调整后营业盈
利。
股权激励费⽤是两种财务⼝径出现明显差异的重要因素。第三季度，公司股权激励费⽤达到
1.551 亿美元，前三季度累计达到 5.417 亿美元。此外，客⼾认股权证摊销也对 GAAP 业绩产⽣影
响。
从⽑利率来看，第三季度 GAAP ⽑利率为 20% ，核⼼⽑利率为 41% 。公司预计第四季度核⼼⽑利

率将进⼀步升⾄ 47% ⾄ 49% ，但这⼀改善仍有待后续财报验证。
对 Cerebras ⽽⾔， AI 推理需求增⻓、云服务客⼾拓展和算⼒基础设施扩张，正在推动公司营收
快速增⻓。但在⾼额资本投⼊和股权激励费⽤的背景下，订单能否按期转化为收⼊，以及规模
扩张能否带来持续的盈利改善，仍是投资者需要关注的问题。

Document 307: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-13)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

Subject: Fed Likely To Hike By 50bps In December

Fed Likely To Hike By 50bps In December

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Fed Likely To Hike By 50bps In
December
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 10 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by liam ward / Unsplash
I. The Fed Has More Work To Do
On October 26, we warned that the Fed could surprise markets with a
25bps rate hike at its October meeting. If the Fed chose to pass on doing
so because the midterm elections were too close, we predicted a 50bps
hike in December. Two days later, the FOMC voted 10-2 to hold the
federal funds rate (FFR) at 3.75%-4.00%, with the two dissenters favoring
a 25bps hike. Yet, in his October 28 presser, Fed Chair Kevin Warsh's

### Page 3

assessment of the economy sounded decidedly hawkish. Economic
growth was robust and broadening, the labor market was in good shape,
inflation remained above the Fed's 2% target, and financial conditions
were hardly tight.
So why did the Fed stand pat?
Warsh argued that financial conditions had tightened, inflation
expectations remained anchored, and stronger productivity growth was
allowing the economy to expand without overheating. With an information-

rich period ahead, the Fed saw little reason to rush. We called that
decision puzzling, comparing it to someone standing in a room with a fire
alarm blaring and smoke pouring under the door, yet insisting on more
evidence before calling the fire department.
Now Fed Governor Christopher Waller is sounding the same alarm.
Speaking in London on Tuesday, Waller said he would support a 25bps
rate hike in December even if inflation improved in October. However, if

October's core CPI increased by around 0.3% m/m or more, as it had in
the previous two months, he argued that a larger move would be
warranted. Specifically, he said that "a 50 basis point hike in the policy
rate should be considered at the December meeting." Waller warned that
moving by only 25bps under those circumstances would jeopardize the
Fed's inflation-fighting credibility.
Economic data released since Waller's remarks suggest that a 50bps hike

in December is becoming increasingly likely. We believe such a move is
warranted, as it would bring the FFR back to our estimated neutral range
of 4.25%-4.50%. Consider the following:
(1) CPI Inflation. October's headline CPI rose by 0.4% m/m, while core
CPI increased 0.3% m/m for the third consecutive month (chart).
Inflationary pressures remain broad-based, with particularly strong
increases in energy, food, and transportation services. Importantly, the

core CPI reading meets Waller's condition for necessitating a 50bps rate
hike in December.

### Page 4

Three forces are contributing to renewed goods inflation: the AI
investment boom, higher energy prices stemming from the Iran war, and
tariffs. In October, prices rose sharply in tariff-sensitive categories such as
furniture & bedding and appliances (chart). However, overall durable
goods prices were essentially unchanged, largely reflecting a 1.2%
decline in used car and truck prices.
Inflationary pressures were also widespread among nondurable goods,

with prices rising 0.5% m/m in October (chart). Recreational reading
materials, housekeeping supplies, footwear, and energy saw particularly
sharp increases. Apparel prices were essentially unchanged, while prices
for computer and software accessories fell.

### Page 5

Another concerning aspect in the inflation data is the persistence of
services inflation, reflecting resilient consumer demand, housing cost
pressures, and the pass-through of higher energy prices. Services prices
rose 0.4% m/m in October, including a 0.4% increase in services
excluding shelter (chart). Postage and delivery, hospital services,
transportation services, and motor vehicle insurance rose sharply.
Owners' equivalent rent also rose 0.3%.

(2) Small Business Sentiment. Echoing the CPI report's fire alarm,
October's NFIB survey showed that the net share of small business
owners planning to raise selling prices jumped to 37%, the highest since
July 2022, while 24% planned to raise worker compensation, the highest
since December 2025 (chart).

### Page 6

(3) Consumer spending. Spending remains remarkably robust. Redbook
same-store retail sales rose 10.1% y/y in the week of November 6, well
above the 2025 full-year average of 5.8% (chart).
(4) Labor market. One of the key reasons consumer spending remains
remarkably strong is the continued strength of the labor market. The latest
ADP data support this assessment. Private employers added an average
of 19,750 jobs per week during the four weeks ending October 24 (chart).

That translates into roughly 86,000 jobs per month, a healthy pace of job
creation that is sufficient to keep the unemployment rate near its current
low level of 4.1%.

### Page 7

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

Document 343: FX volatility in 2027_ In need of shock therapy

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX volatility in 2027_ In need of shock therapy.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Why is FX volatility so low? Even a 6% fall in the Nasdaq barely
budges USD/JPY these days. Have FX markets found a general
equilibrium, or is there some kind of separation between
geopolitics and macro? We think the answer lies more in the
structure of FX trading and do not rule out jump risk if the
shock is big enough

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole and Dmitry Dolgin
Whatever happened to FX volatility?
Despite wars in the Middle East and Continental Europe, an energy-
driven inﬂation spike and growing fears over an AI bubble, FX
volatility is close to its lowest levels in over a decade. If moves in
spot exchange rates are the ultimate driver of traded volatility, then
the recent stasis in spot USD/JPY in the face of a 6% drop in the
Nasdaq tells the story. In short, if exchange rates aren’t moving,
why should the market pay up for volatility?
It is not as though FX volatility is low in all markets. AI concerns
have seen equity volatility spike, while surging energy prices,
Your privacy is important

### Page 2

central bank tightening, and fears about debt sustainability have
all seen interest rate volatility remain elevated.
FX volatility has remained muted
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
The lack of spot FX movement or spot follow-through cannot
simply be blamed on some kind of separation between geopolitics
and macro. This year’s war in Iran has driven a massive hawkish
repricing at the short end of yield curves and turned easing cycles
on their heads. And in this outlook, we argue that if there is one

theme that FX markets should take seriously, it’s global monetary
policy cycles.
Any pickup in FX volatility more likely from
equities than bonds
One of our key calls for 2027 is that we don’t expect a signiﬁcant
pickup in FX volatility. Markets have built up considerable tolerance
for US policy and geopolitical headlines, and may be even more
willing to fade US political risk if a Democrat-led Congress and
weaker Republican support constrain US President Donald Trump’s

room for aggressive policy action.
If volatility does break higher, we think equities are a more likely
trigger than rates. Historical evidence supports that view, with
equity volatility consistently showing greater pass-through into FX
than rates volatility (chart below).
Your privacy is important

### Page 3

Sticky global inﬂation and prolonged monetary tightening are
more plausible risks than a major equity selloﬀ. Yet we don’t think
even a high-inﬂation scenario would be enough to recreate 2022-
2023 levels of FX volatility. Policy rates entered the Iran war from
much higher levels than at the end of the pandemic, while ﬁscal
support is smaller and labour markets are looser. The ingredients
for another 2022-23-style rates shock simply look less potent.

Greater FX shock potential from equities
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Structural factors: Growth in ﬁnancial
assets playing a role?
When thinking about factors that could be suppressing volatility in
spot exchange rates, we suspect the growth in the asset
management industry could be playing a role. Both assets under
management and cross-border positions have continued to surge
over recent years. Using World Bank and Bank for International
Settlements (BIS) data, we now estimate that global holdings of

equities and bonds are worth around 300% of global GDP. As the BIS
reported back in 2025, FX trading volumes were about 70 times the
volume of global trade in 2025, roughly double the ratio of 1992.
These ﬁnancial FX ﬂows, including portfolio hedging and
rebalancing, can be an important source of FX liquidity when they
are gradual and diversiﬁed. But correlated hedging or rebalancing
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

### Page 4

can amplify FX moves. The latter was the case during the Trump
tariﬀ shock in March/April 2025.
Stock of global equity and ﬁxed income
holdings
Source: World Bank, BIS, ING
Structural factors: FX engineering playing a
role?
One of the big trends in FX trading over recent years has been the
‘internalisation’ of FX ﬂows – the concept being that a bank can
deliver steadier, better pricing for customers by oﬀsetting client
ﬂow with other internal client ﬂow/hedging activity without having

to go to external liquidity venues.
We suspect there is quite a lot of overstatement of the extent of
‘internalisation’ out there, where banks may be including dealing
on ‘lit’ venues (transparent order books) as part of their
internalisation activity. Undoubtedly, the advent of non-bank
liquidity providers to lit venues has been a major contributor to the
depth of market pricing, as has the growth of algorithmic pricing
engines. The design of these algo pricing engines, which pull back

from putting large volumes through in thin conditions, has
probably contributed to calmer conditions, too. Indeed, it seems
that when volatility conditions pick up, the buy-side prefers to use
algos to execute rather than paying the wider spreads associated
with principal liquidity transfers.

### Page 5

This publication has been prepared by ING solely for information purposes
irrespective of a particular user's means, ﬁnancial situation or investment
But before we conclude that FX volatility is dead, evidence shows
that major events – Liberation Day in April 2025, or the start of the
Iran war in February 2026 – can still have a big say in FX markets.
Below, we show some data on the depth of the EUR/USD market
starting in 2025. The chart shows the impact on EUR/USD, in USD

pips, one minute after a trade has gone through. We show the
impact of four size buckets, equally weighted: $0-1m, $1-5m, $5-
10m and $10-30m.
Big events do still impact market depth, which could be due to
some non-banks pulling liquidity from lit venues.
So, while the above factors could be contributing to lower volatility,
our conclusion is that volatility is not dead and jump risk or fat tails
will still be a fact of life in FX markets.

deterioration

园产业

 换手率仍处低位，资金参与热度尚未出现整体性回升。整体来看，本月
跌幅收窄或更多体现为前期调整后的局部修复，短期市场表现仍可能受到市场情绪、
流动性环境及基金估值消化进度等多重因素的综合影响。中长期来看，园区资产的结
构性分化格局尚未改变；未来城市更新项目与
 REITs
 发行范围的有机衔接，或将为板
块提供新的发展动能。其中，运营体系成熟、租约结构稳定且区位条件较优的项目有
望
持续受益
。

### Page 10

中航证券
研究所
发布 
基金实现上涨
。板块内部来看，中金山东高速
 REIT
、平安广州广河
 REIT
 、浙商沪杭
甬
 REIT
 等实现上涨，平安宁波交投
 REIT
 、东方红隧道股份高速公路
 REIT
、易方达
深高速
 REIT
 等有所回调，
个基
表现有所分化。交易活跃度方面，东方红隧道股份高
速公路
 REIT
 月度换手处于板块较高水平，工银河北高速
 REIT
 换手率仍处低位。中
长期来看，
随着
收费公路管理养护及经营期限届满后的制度安排逐步明晰，有助于稳
定市场对特许经营类路产现金流的预期；交通强国和现代综合交通运输体系建设，也
为存量路产的持续运营提供长期支撑。具备较好区位条件、车流量增长潜力和较高运
营效率的项目，其稳定分派特征和成熟路产价值或更易获得长期资金关注。

 近期新品上市数量较多，
 对于新上市基金，应重点

1.
交通基础设施
 REITs
 具有一定的垄断性和刚需属性，具备较强的稳定性。板块
内部因区域经济与产业结构差异呈现分化，部分路产车流量增长承压，而核心城市群
路产表现相对稳健，
 10
 月板块整体小幅调整，但内部表现并非一致走弱，区域位置、
路网结构和运营差异仍是
 个基
定价的重要依据。中长期看，交通强国建设和收费公路
相关制度完善，有助于为优质路产提供相对稳定的发展环境。建议优先关注具有区域
垄断地位、路网位置较为核心、车流量和通行费收入可预期性较强的项目。

 "

 REITs
。

 存在

园区基础设施
 REITs
。板块经过持续调整后，部分项目的估值压力有所释放。

可控趋势；
 "
十五五

时期老旧街区、厂区改造任务明确，城市更新项目纳入
 REITs
 发

衡业态组合的园区资产，其估值修复空间值得重点关注。

Document 442: Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 19
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Why hasn’t there been a major 
financial crisis in recent years?

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

Peter Sidorov, CFA 
Senior Economist

Camilla Siazon 
Research Analyst

November 3, 2026 
— The economist Hyman Minsky warned, in essence, that “stability is destabilising”. He 
argued that a lengthy period of financial stability could be problematic, as it induced 
risk-taking and complacency amongst investors, laying the foundation for the next 
crisis. For example, lenders relax their terms and borrowers are more willing to extend 
themselves, as nobody wants to miss out. This dynamic makes economies inherently 
prone to financial panics and boom-bust cycles. 
— But since the 2008 crisis, and the subsequent sovereign crisis in the Eurozone, we 
have yet to see another financial crisis happen on a scale that had a lasting market 
and macroeconomic impact. There have been clear moments of concern, as in early 
2023 with the regional bank turmoil, but nothing that has caused a lasting impact. So 
this led us to ask why. 
— In this paper, we examine why the post-GFC era has seen fewer financial crises. 
Explanations include the reforms made after 2008 to avoid a repeat, the lower 
proportion of financial assets held by the banking system, as well as the speedier 
reaction of policymakers when wobbles have occurred. We then consider a few 
candidates that could cause the next crisis, including AI vulnerabilities, private credit, 
and stretched fiscal positions. 
— Ultimately, financial crises haven’t been abolished, but the reforms of recent years 
have made it harder for shocks to propagate through the traditional banking system. 
Moreover, policymakers have become far more inclined to intervene when initial signs 
of stress have emerged.

### Page 2

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 1: What is a financial crisis, and what causes them?
There isn’t a single technical definition of a “financial crisis”, and they come in many
different types. Commonly, we think of banking crises like 2008, but they can also
encompass currency/balance of payments crises, debt crises and other forms too.
Given currency and sovereign crises have been more common in emerging markets
(the UK's ERM crisis in 1992 and the euro crisis being the two most notable DM

occurrences), historical analysis in developed economies has tended to focus
heavily on banking crises. Non-bank funding or market-liquidity crises are often not
categorised separately in the literature, but they have become more common in
recent years. This includes the "dash-for-cash" stress at the onset of the Covid
pandemic in March 2020 and the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK.
But for all the different types of financial crisis, there are common themes between

them. Often, they are accompanied by asset bubbles that burst in a non-linear
fashion. We generally see a severe tightening in credit conditions, which in turn
flows through into a broader economic contraction. Frequently, there are
contagion-like dynamics that can be difficult to stop. And in the modern world, they
usually require policymakers to step in, stabilising the situation with interventions.
At risk of oversimplification, we note four factors that determine the emergence and

the severity of a financial crisis. The first three are well-established in the financial
literature, sometimes pooled together into a build-up and a crisis phase
(Brunnermeier & Oehmke 2013), while the fourth has become increasingly
important over the past 15-20 years:
1.  Underlying imbalances. Bubbles and financial imbalances build during
the run-up phase, leaving accumulated risks that materialise when the
crisis erupts. This is the build-up typically described by the early phases of

Kindleberger's model of financial crises.
2.  Trigger. This is the shock that turns an existing vulnerability into clear
financial stress. These are often very difficult to predict and we mostly leave
these outside the focus of this report.
3.  Amplification and financial system fragility. Various factors determine
whether a shock propagates through the financial system, and the
intensity of that shock. These include leverage, liquidity and maturity

mismatches, interconnectedness, and the concentration of exposures.
Other channels in the financial system can then amplify the shock,
including bank runs, fire sales and margin calls, and negative net worth
effects, sovereign–bank loops etc.
4.  Intervention/policy response. This has been increasingly important in
recent decades as the size and scope of policy response to crises has
increased. The outright size can vary, and intervention can occur across

several dimensions, ranging from direct intervention in financial markets,
to counter-cyclical macroeconomic policy that mitigates the impact on the
real economy.
Clearly, these factors are not independent and the interaction between them is what
determines the severity of a financial crisis.

### Page 3

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
One important aspect is the joint role that underlying imbalances and amplification
play. While theoretical models such as Diamond & Dybvig highlight the potential for
a bank run that triggers a broad crisis, empirical research shows that in practice, it
is exceedingly rare for a bank run to lead to bank failure in the absence of an
underlying solvency issue (Correia, Luck and Verner 2026). In other words, the

presence of amplification channels alone does not lead to a crisis.
Conversely, in the absence of amplification, it is possible to get significant
turbulence in asset prices, but without a financial crisis. For instance, a bear market,
wider credit spreads, or even a recession do not in and of themselves imply there
is a financial crisis. This was seen clearly with the bursting of the dot com bubble,
a severe equity decline but not a financial crisis, though it did cause a recession. At

the same time, in the chart below, we can see several US equity bear markets that
took place outside of recessions. On other occasions, however, a “Minsky
Moment” can then happen, where even a seemingly minor event can trigger a high-
profile incident that cascades across the financial system.
Figure 1: S&P 500 drawdowns over time, with shaded recessions showing select episodes when the index fell over 20%
without coinciding with a recession
Source :Bloomberg Finance LP, NBER, Deutsche Bank Research

Looking back through history, we can see how variation in these factors has led to
different regimes over time in terms of frequency/severity of financial factors.

### Page 4

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 2. Why have financial crises been more or less likely
in certain periods of history?
1. Pre-WWII: More frequent financial instability, albeit under a very
different system.
Financial instability was a recurrent feature of the pre-WWII era. During the 19th
and early 20th centuries, there were frequent panics that often led to major slumps.
For instance, the Panic of 1873 sparked a period often known as the “Long

Depression”, which extended into the 1890s for many countries.
In part, financial panics occurred more frequently because policy operated under
very different settings. For instance, the Gold Standard as the monetary system
constrained a countercyclical policy response and transmitted deflation
internationally, while falling prices increased real debt burdens. Moreover, the
Federal Reserve wasn’t established until 1913, and the lack of an effective lender of

last resort meant there was a private intervention during the US Panic of 1907. The
first wave of globalisation also brought new vulnerabilities. Between 1870 and
1913, many emerging economies accumulated debts denominated in gold or
foreign currency, leaving them exposed when capital inflows reversed or exchange
rates weakened.
The biggest and most famous crisis occurred in 1929, when the Wall Street Crash
of that year sparked the Great Depression. The length and depth of that crisis helped

to stimulate the study of modern macroeconomics, with huge efforts to prevent a
repeat over the years ahead. Indeed, the failures of the response to the Great
Depression played a large role in the much swifter response to the 2008 financial
crisis, helping to avoid a second Great Depression.
That experience established much of the modern financial safety net that exists to
this day. For instance in the US, the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation was

established in 1933. The US and many other countries came off the Gold Standard
as well in this period. There were also regulatory reforms, and the Glass-Steagall Act
separated out commercial from investment banking.
2. 1944-1971: The Bretton Woods era coincided with fewer financial
crises
Around the end of WWII, economic policymakers sought to create an international
monetary system for the post-war era. The relative financial calm that followed

reflected a combination of restrictions on international capital movement, tightly
regulated domestic financial systems, and a system of fixed exchange rates that
aimed to provide stability and prevent the competitive devaluations that occurred
in the 1930s. This saw exchange rates pegged against the US Dollar, which in turn
was convertible to gold at $35 per ounce.
At the same time, capital controls helped preserve monetary policy flexibility, while

maintaining the system of fixed exchange rates. These controls had several
downsides but they also insulated countries from “sudden stop” crises that can
occur, when capital rapidly flees a country. Moreover, capital controls prevented
the rapid buildup of cross-border systemic leverage. So it was more difficult to
generate a global financial crisis in the same way.

### Page 5

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Figure 2: Gold price (USD/oz, log scale)

In the Bretton Woods

Source : Finaeon, Deutsche Bank
Alongside capital controls, there were also domestic reforms in the aftermath of the
Great Depression, which sought to avoid a repeat. As mentioned earlier, the US
Glass-Steagall Act of 1933 separated commercial banking from investment

banking. Another example of the era’s regulated financial system in the US was
Regulation Q, which imposed maximum interest rates that commercial banks
could pay on deposits. That meant depositors could not search for yield and cause
a run on deposits as easily. Another rationale was that with institutions not
competing for deposits, it would avoid a compression in the margin between the
rates banks paid on their lending and borrowing, which would disincentivise a push

into riskier investments.
Finally, economic growth was also stronger in this period, with the period between
WWII and the 1973 oil shock often described as the “Golden Age”. This played a role
in avoiding financial crises too, as faster growth helped keep debt default rates
down, avoiding the kind of “trigger” events above that might spark a financial crisis.
Coupled with financial repression, strong growth also helped to bring down the
high post-war public debt burdens.

Over time however, the peg between the dollar and gold found itself under growing
strain. The economist Robert Triffin identified the “Triffin dilemma”. Supplying the
world with dollar reserves resulted in outstanding dollar liabilities around the world
that exceeded the gold reserves in the US, weakening confidence that dollars could
ultimately be redeemed for gold. Conversely, restricting dollar supply to protect
those reserves and preserve convertibility risked depriving the system of the

liquidity needed to support trade and growth.
These tensions intensified in the late 1960s amid expansionary US policies,
including spending on the Vietnam War and Great Society programmes. The
continued drain in US gold reserves eventually led the US to suspend the
convertibility of the US Dollar into gold, effectively ending the Bretton Woods
system. Initially, there were attempts to bring some kind of fixed-exchange rate
system back, but the world of fiat currencies (with no link to a precious metal)

persists to this day.

### Page 6

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
3. 1971-2008: Financial crises become more frequent after the
breakdown of Bretton Woods to the Global Financial Crisis
After the breakdown of the Bretton Woods system, financial crises became a more
regular occurrence. While the end of the gold convertibility did not by itself trigger
future crises, it opened the door for wider financial liberalisation, with the resulting
rise in capital mobility and looser domestic financial regulation then helping to

facilitate a global build-up of imbalances.
The 1980s and 1990s were a period of frequent crisis events. The transition away
from capital controls meant that capital could flow more freely across borders.
However, this could also cause a “sudden stop” if investors suddenly pulled their
capital out of a country, contributing to bank runs and currency collapses.
Moreover, many emerging markets borrowed in foreign currencies like the US
Dollar, meaning that a depreciation in their own currency would see this USD-

denominated debt surge in local currency terms. This meant it was a particular
problem if US interest rates were increasing, as it became harder for many
emerging markets to repay their debts. This was a contributing factor behind the
Latin American debt crisis in the early 1980s. And the rate hikes also had domestic
ramifications, being a contributing factor behind the Savings and Loan crisis in the
United States.
With capital flowing more freely, that also played a role in the 1994 Mexican Peso

Crisis, and the 1997 Asian Financial Crisis. A sudden reversal in capital flows led to
crisis and ultimately recession in many countries. The following year, the Russian
financial crisis then took place, and fears grew in the United States about the wider
ramifications, as the hedge fund Long-Term Capital Management (LTCM) came
under pressure. Indeed, this period saw 75bps of pre-emptive rate cuts from the
Federal Reserve.
This volatility also saw the Fed proactively seek to step in and smooth out the

economic volatility. This was broadly successful, but in the process also helped
engender complacency and a further buildup of leverage. Indeed, after the dot com
bubble burst, the federal funds rate was cut to just 1% in mid-2003, where it
remained until mid-2004.
This period saw clear optimism that another Great Depression was highly unlikely.
For instance, Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Lucas argued in his 2003 AEA
Presidential Address that “macroeconomics in this original sense has succeeded:

Its central problem of depression prevention has been solved, for all practical
purposes, and has in fact been solved for many decades.”
However, as we now know, the 2008 financial crisis occurred, driving the biggest
global downturn since the Great Depression of the 1930s. It turned out that Minsky
had been correct, and a lengthy period without a crisis had once again helped to
induce complacency, laying the foundations for a repeat.

3 November 2026

Section 3: The Post-2008 world: Why have we not had
another big financial crisis?
Since the Global Financial Crisis of 2008, we have thankfully avoided a major
financial crisis. Despite clear moments of stress, most notably around the collapse
of SVB in March 2023, we have not seen a similar wave of bank collapses on that
scale. There are several components to this.
First, post-GFC deleveraging cleared out risks in the system, with countries and
sectors that had excess indebtedness before the GFC experiencing pronounced

declines in leverage.
Second, there have been reforms explicitly designed to avoid a repeat of 2008.
After the crisis happened, there was a clear recognition that gaps in regulation had
exacerbated the crisis, leading to effects that cascaded across the financial system.
For instance, the Basel III reforms required banks to hold more equity, with a
minimum requirement for Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital. They also
introduced the liquidity coverage ratio, which requires banks to hold enough high-

quality liquid assets to ensure they can cover their net cash outflows under a 30-day
stressed scenario. In addition, the net stable funding ratio was brought in, requiring
banks to have sufficient stable funding that exceeded the required amount of stable
funding for a one-year stressed scenario.
At the national level, further reforms were also introduced. In the US, the Dodd-
Frank Act was introduced, with measures including the Volcker Rule that restricted

banks from engaging in proprietary trading, and required the Federal Reserve to
perform an annual stress test of large institutions. Meanwhile in the European
Union, which also experienced a sovereign debt crisis, reforms included a “Single
Supervisory Mechanism”, whereby banking supervision was brought under the
ECB, ensuring harmonised regulatory standards.

### Page 8

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
Third, the share of financial assets held by banks has fallen since the 2008 crisis.
This hasn’t eliminated risk, but commercial banks are funded by retail deposits that
can quickly fly. By contrast, non-bank financial institutions (NBFIs), e.g. hedge
funds, are not as prone to these bank run dynamics. For instance, a private credit
closed-ended fund is not one where investors can simply redeem their funds on

demand.
We can see this shift in the data. Looking at the FSB’s group of 21 plus the Eurozone,
we can see that the share of assets held by banks has fallen from a peak of 46% in
2008, to 38% in 2024. By contrast, the share held by NBFIs has risen from 43% to
51%.
Figure 5: Share of Assets of Financial Corporations (FSB 21 + EA Group)
